[[c12_2]]
== 12.2 Past Tense of Adjectives and Nouns

This module introduces how to use nouns and adjectives in the past tense in Japanese, demonstrating how to effectively describe past situations and qualities in conversation.

=== Introduction to the past tense of adjectives and nouns

In <<c11_2, Module 11.2>>, we introduced the three groups of adjectives in Japanese: *i-adjectives, irregular i-adjectives and na-adjectives*. In this module, we’ll show you how to conjugate each group into the *past tense*, including both *affirmative and negative forms*, with clear examples for each.

Additionally, we will cover *how to express the past tense for nouns*, as nouns in Japanese follow the same conjugation pattern as *na-adjectives* when forming affirmative and negative statements in the past tense.

[width="100%",cols=",20%,20%,20%,20%",options="header",]
|===
|Type |Present affirmative |Past affirmative |Present negative |Past negative

|*1. I-adjectives*  {BR}(e.g., おいしい)
|おいしいです  {BR}(oishii desu: it’s tasty) 
|おいし**かったです**  {BR}(oishi katta desu) 
|おいし**くない** *です* {BR}(oishiku nai desu) 
|おいし**くなかったです**  {BR}(oishiku nakatta desu)

1.2+|*2. Irregular i-adjectives*  {BR}(e.g., かっこいい) 
|いいです  {BR}(ii desu: it’s good) 
|*よかったです*  {BR}(yokatta desu) 
|*よくないです*  {BR}(yoku nai desu) 
|*よくなかったです*  {BR}(yoku nakatta desu)
|かっこいいです  {BR}(kakkoii desu: it’s cool/good-looking) 
|かっこ**よかったです**  {BR}(kakko yokatta desu) 
|かっこ**よくないです**  {BR}(kakko yoku nai desu) 
|かっこ**よくなかったです**  {BR}(kakko yoku nakatta desu)

|*3. Na-adjectives*  {BR}(e.g., きれいな)
|きれいです  {BR}(kirei  desu: it’s beautiful/clean) 
|きれい**でした**  {BR}(kirei deshita) 
|きれい**じゃない** *です*  {BR}(kirei janai desu) 
|きれい**じゃなかったです**  {BR}(kirei janakatta desu)

|*4. Nouns*  {BR}(e.g., 学生 [がくせい])
|学生です  {BR}(gakusei desu__:__ I’m a student) 
|学生**でした**  {BR}(gakusei deshita) 
|学生**じゃないです**  {BR}(gakusei janai desu) 
|学生**じゃなかったです**  {BR}(gakusei janakatta desu)
|===

==== 1. I-adjectives

*Affirmative past-tense form* ::

•  To make an i-adjective affirmative in the past tense, replace the final *い* with *かったです* (katta desu).
•  Example: Non-past: *楽（たの）しいです* (tanoshii desu) → Past: *楽しかったです* (tanoshikatta desu) – ‘was fun’

*Negative past-tense form* ::

•  To make an i-adjective negative in the past tense, replace the final *い* with *くなかったです* (kunakatta desu).
•  Example: Non-past: *楽しくないです* (tanoshiku nai desu) → Past: *楽しくなかったです* (tanoshiku nakatta desu) – ‘was not fun’

*Examples:*

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 日本（にほん）の旅行（りょこう）は**楽しかったです**。 +
 _Nihon no ryokou wa hontouni *tanoshi katta desu*_.
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/07/Chap12-2-L1.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| The trip to Japan *was fun*.
|===

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| そのテストは**難（むずか）しくなかったです**。 +
 _Sono tesuto wa *muzukashiku nakatta desu*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/07/Chap12-2-L2.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| That test *wasn’t difficult*.
|===

==== 2. Irregular i-adjectives

*Affirmative past-tense form* ::

•  The irregular i-adjective *いい* (ii: good) becomes *よかったです* (yokatta desu) in the affirmative past tense.
•  Example: *いいです* (ii desu) → *よかったです* (yokatta desu) – ‘was good’

*Negative past-tense form* ::

•  The irregular i-adjective *いい* (ii) becomes *よくなかったです* (yokunakatta desu) in the negative past tense.
•  Example: *よくないです* (yokunai desu) → *よくなかったです* (yokunakatta desu) – ‘was not good’

*Examples:*

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| あのホテルはあまり**よくなかったですね**。 +
 _Ano hoteru wa amari *yokuna katta desu ne*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/07/Chap12-2-L3.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| That hotel *wasn’t* really *good, was it*?
|===

==== 3. Na-adjectives

*Affirmative past-tense form* ::

•  To make a na-adjective affirmative in the past tense, remove *な* and add *でした* (deshita).
•  Example: *きれいな* (kireina) → *きれいでした* (kirei deshita) – ‘was beautiful/clean’

*Negative past-tense form* ::

•  To make a na-adjective negative in the past tense, remove *な* and add *じゃなかったです* (janakatta desu).
•  Example: *きれいじゃないです* (kirei janai desu) → *きれいじゃなかったです* (kirei janakatta desu) – ‘was not beautiful/clean’


*Examples:*

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| この公園（こうえん）はとても**きれいでした**。 +
 _Kono kouen wa totemo *kirei deshita*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/07/Chap12-2-L4.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| This park *was* very *beautiful*.
|===

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 昨日（きのう）は**元気（げんき）じゃなかったです**。 +
 _kinou wa *genki janakatta desu.*_
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/07/Chap12-2-L5.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| I *wasn’t well* yesterday.
|===

==== 4. Nouns

*Affirmative Past-Tense Form* ::

•  To make a noun affirmative in the past tense, add *でした* (deshita) after the noun.
•  Example: 学生 (gakusei) → *学生でした* (gakusei deshita) – ‘was a student’

*Negative Past-Tense Form* ::

•  To make a noun negative in the past tense, use *じゃなかったです* (janakatta desu).
•  Example: 学生じゃないです (gakusei janai desu) → *学生じゃなかったです* (gakusei janakatta desu) – ‘was not a student’

*Examples:*

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 私（わたし）のお兄（にい）さんは**学生でした。** +
 _Watashi no oniisan wa *gakusei deshita*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/07/Chap12-2-L6.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| My older brother *was a (university) student.*
|===

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 私はいい**学生じゃなかったです。** +
 _Watashi wa ii gakusei *janakatta desu*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/07/Chap12-2-L7.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| I *was not a* good *(university) student.*
|===

*NOTE:* In this sentence, *いい* *(ii: good)* is an *adjective modifying the noun 学生 (gakusei: student).* The *negative past-tense form* is applied to *the entire noun phrase* *いい学生* (good student), rather than *いい* itself. So, いい学生じゃないです (not a good student) becomes いい学生じゃなかったです (was not a good student).

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
|✔ *私はいい学生じゃなかったです。* +
 _Watashi wa ii gakusei janakatta desu._ +
| *I was not a good (university) student.*

|✖ *私はよくなかった学生じゃないです。*
| (_Incorrect!_)
|===

NOTE: 「私はよくなかった学生じゃないです」 语法上成立，但意思非常绕，双重否定容易引起误解：“我不是一个不好的学生。”

=== Usage notes

In Japanese, past-tense adjectives are not used directly to modify nouns. Instead, they are used in separate sentences or clauses to describe past states or qualities:


[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| おいしくなかったご飯（はん）を食（た）べました。 +
 _Oishiku nakatta gohan o tabe mashita_.
| *Incorrect usage*
2+^| ↓
2+^|*Correct usage*
| ごはんを食べました。*おいしくなかったです*。 +
 _Gohan o tabemashita. *Oishiku nakatta desu*._
| I ate a meal (or rice). It *wasn’t tasty*.
|===

=== Exercise 1

[[h5p-102]]
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/japanese/wp-admin/admin-ajax.php?action=h5p_embed&id=102[Chapter 12 Past Tense Nouns and Adjectives]
 
Drag the words into the correct boxes
(よかった・おいしかった・たのしかった・げんきだった・いそがしかった・しんせつだった)

1. いい - *よかった*
2. おいしい - *おいしかった*
3. 楽しい - *たのしかった*
4. 忙しい - *いそがしかった*
5. 親切な - *しんせつだった*
6. 元気な　- *げんきだった*

Choose the correct form for this scenario. Expressing “The party was fun, but it was busy”:

A. パーティーは楽しいでした。でも、忙しいでした。
B. パーティーは楽しかったです。でも、忙しかったです。 [.QA]*true*
C. パーティーは楽しいです。でも、忙しいでした。
D. パーティーは楽しかった。でも、忙しかった。

Hints: * 楽（たの）しい * 忙（いそが）しい

Saying “The staff at the hotel were very kind”.

A. ホテルのスタッフはとても親切です。
B. ホテルのスタッフはとても親切でした。 [.QA]*true*
C. ホテルのスタッフはとても親切なでした。
D. ホテルのスタッフはとても親切だった。

Hints: * 親切（しんせつ）

Fill in the blanks with the correct form of the word in hiragana.

1. 昨日（きのう）の仕事（しごと）は [.QA]*いそがしかったです*。(いそがしい)
   Yesterday’s work was busy.
2. パーティーは [.QA]*たのしかったです*。(たのしい)
   The party was fun.
3. その学生はとても [.QA]*しんせつでした*。(しんせつな)
   That student was very kind.
4. メルボルンのレストランはとても [.QA]*たかかったです*。(たかい)
   The restaurant in Melbourne was very expensive.
5. 東京（とうきょう）はとても [.QA]*にぎやかでした*。(にぎやかな)
   Tokyo was very lively.
6. 私のお姉さんはあまり [.QA]*げんきじゃなかったです*。（げんきな)
   My older sister wasn’t very energetic.
7. 昨日のテストはあまり [.QA]*よくなかったです*。（いい)
   Yesterday’s test wasn’t very good.

Complete the dialogue by filling in the blanks with the correct forms of the given words.
(でした・でした・親切でした・どうでしたか・よかったです・楽しかったです・いそがしかったです) 

•  A: 昨日（きのう）のパーティーは [.QA]*どうでしたか*。
   How was yesterday’s party?
•  B: とても [.QA]*楽しかったです*。でも、少（すこ）し疲（つか）れました。
   It was very fun. But I got a little tired.
•  A: そうですか。私（わたし）は昨日（きのう）バイト [.QA]*でした*。 [.QA]*いそがしかったです*。
   I see. I had a part-time job yesterday. It was busy.
•  B: 大変（たいへん） [.QA]*でした* ね。
   That sounds tough.
•  A: でも、お客さんは、とても [.QA]*親切でした*。
   But the customers were very kind.
•  B: それは [.QA]*よかったです* ね。
   That’s great to hear.

Hints: * いそがし・い  4 【忙しい】（形）。* どう  1 【〈如何〉】（副）。* どう でも  0  1 【〈如何〉でも】（副）。* とて も  0 【▼迚も】（副）非常に.


[[c12_3]]
== 12.3 Past Tense of Verbs

This module demonstrates how to effectively use past-tense verbs in Japanese to describe past actions, events and experiences, perfect for narrating personal experiences or recounting past events such as a trip to Japan.

=== Introduction to the past tense of verbs

In <<c06_2, Module 6.2>>, we explored verbs and their conjugations in the polite form – *ます* form – focusing on the present and future (or non-past) tense. Building upon that foundation, this module introduces past-tense verbs in Japanese. Verbs in the past tense convey actions or states that occurred in the past. Let’s delve into how the past tense is formed for different types of verbs and how past-tense verbs are used in questions and sentences.

=== Forming past-tense verbs 

In Japanese, the polite past tense of many verbs is straightforward once you know the *ます* form. *ました* is the affirmative past-tense ending, and *ませんでした* is the negative past-tense ending. Here’s how the different verb types – ru-verbs, u-verbs and irregular verbs – are conjugated:

==== 1. Ru-verbs

===== Affirmative past-tense forms

To form the affirmative past tense of ru-verbs, replace *ます* with *ました*.

*Example* ::

•  *食（た）べます* (tabe masu) — ‘to eat’
•  *食べました* (tabe mashita) — ‘ate’

===== Negative past-tense forms

To form the negative past tense of ru-verbs, replace *ません* with *ませんでした*.

*Example* ::

•  *食べません* (tabe masen) — ‘do/does not eat’
•  *食べませんでした* (tabe masen deshita) — ‘did/does not eat’


==== 2. U-verbs

===== Affirmative past-tense forms

Just as with ru-verbs, you can form the affirmative past tense of u-verbs by simply changing *ます* to *ました*. Here’s a table showing the *ます* and *ました* forms for each type of u-verb. This is also a great opportunity to review how to conjugate u-verbs into the *ます* form:

[%autowidth,cols="^,^,^,^,^",options="header",]
|===
|Ending |Dictionary Form |Conjugation: Change /u/ to /i/ |Non-Past Tense (Polite) |Past Tense (Polite)
|う |買う (kau: to buy)      |う (u) → い (i)     |買います |買いました 
|つ |持つ (motsu: to hold)   |つ (tsu) → ち (chi) |持ちます |持ちました 
|る |切る (kiru: to cut)     |る (ru) → り (ri)   |切ります |切りました 
|む |飲む (nomu: to drink)   |む (mu) → み (mi)   |飲みます |飲みました 
|ぶ |遊ぶ (asobu: to play)   |ぶ (bu) → び (bi)   |遊びます |遊びました 
|ぬ |死ぬ (shinu: to die)    |ぬ (nu) → に (ni)   |死にます |死にました 
|く |書く (kaku: to write)   |く (ku) → き (ki)   |書きます |書きました 
|ぐ |泳ぐ (oyogu: to swim)   |ぐ (gu) → ぎ (gi)   |泳ぎます |泳ぎました 
|す |話す (hanasu: to speak) |す (su) → し (shi)  |話します |話しました 
|===

// (kai masu)     (kai mashita)
// (mochi masu)   (mochi mashita)
// (kiri masu)    (kiri mashita)
// (nomi masu)    (nomi mashita)
// (asobi masu)   (asobi mashita)
// (shini masu)   (shini mashita)
// (kaki masu)    (kaki mashita)
// (oyogi masu)   (oyogi mashita)
// (hanashi masu) (hanashi mashita)

===== Negative Form

Just as with ru-verbs, you can form the negative past tense of u-verbs by simply changing *ません* to *ませんでした*.

*Example* ::

•  *話しません* (hanashi masen) – ‘do/does not speak’
•  *話しませんでした* (hanashi masen deshita) – ‘did not speak’


==== 3. Irregular verbs

Irregular verbs have unique forms in *ました*, which differ from their *ます* forms. Here’s a table of the affirmative non-past and past polite forms of irregular verbs:

===== Affirmative past-tense forms

[%autowidth,cols="^,^,^,^",options="header",]
|===
|Dictionary Form  |Meaning |Non-Past Tense (Polite) |Past Tense (Polite)
|来（く）る (kuru) |to come |来（き）ます (ki masu)   |来（き）ました (ki mashita)
|する (suru)      |to do    |します (shi masu)       |しました (shi mashita)
|勉強（べんきょう）する (benkyou suru) |to study |勉強します (benkyou shi masu) |勉強しました (benkyou shi mashita)
|===

===== Negative past-tense forms

Just as with other verb types, you can form the negative past tense of irregular verbs by simply changing *ません* to *ませんでした*.

*Example* ::

•  *来ません* (ki masen) — ‘do/does not come’
•  *来ませんでした* (ki masen deshita) — ‘did not come’


=== Using past-tense verbs in sentences

Using past-tense verbs in Japanese allows you to describe actions or states that occurred in the past. Here are some commonly used sentence patterns with past-tense verbs:

==== 1. Affirmative past-tense sentences

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 去年（きょねん）、日本（にほん）に**行（い）きました**。 +
 _Kyonen, Nihon ni *iki mashita*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-3-L1.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| He *went* to Japan last year.
|===


*NOTE*: The time reference 去年 does not take the particle に because it indicates a relative time frame rather than a specific point in time (i.e., ‘last year’ could be 1996, 2024 or any other year, depending on when you say it). For further details on when to use or omit に with time expressions, see <<c06_2, Module 6.2>>.


[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 京都（きょうと）で古（ふる）いお寺（てら）を**見（み）ました**。 +
 _Kyouto de furui otera o *mi mashita*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-3-L2.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| I *saw* old temples in Kyoto. (Or: I saw an old temple in Kyoto.)
|===

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 疲（つか）れましたから、ホテル（ほてる）で**休（やす）みました。** +
 _Tsukare mashita kara, hoteru de *yasumi mashita*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-3-L3.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| I *rested* at the hotel because I was tired.
|===


==== 2. Negative past-tense sentences

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 大阪（おおさか）に**行（い）きませんでした**。 +
 _Oosaka ni *iki* *masen deshita*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-3-L4.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| I *didn’t* *go* to Osaka.
|===


[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 富士山（ふじさん）の写真（しゃしん）を**撮（と）りませんでした。 +
 _Fuji-san no shashin o *tori masen deshita*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-3-L5.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| I *didn’t* *take* photos of Mt. Fuji.
|===

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 昨日（きのう）、友達（ともだち）は学校（がっこう）に**来（き）ませんでした**。 +
 _Kinou tomodachi wa gakkou ni *ki masen deshita*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-3-L6.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| My friend *didn’t come* to school yesterday.
|===


*NOTE*: The time reference 昨日 does not take the particle に because it indicates a relative time frame rather than a specific point in time. For further details on when to use or omit に with time expressions, see <<c06_2, Module 6.2>>.


==== 3. Questions in the past tense

*Closed-ended questions:*

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| ドアを**閉（し）めましたか**。 +
 _Doa o *shime* *mashita ka*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-3-L7.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| *Did* you *close* the door**?**

| スマホを**充電（じゅうでん）しましたか**。 +
 _Sumaho o *juuden shi mashita ka*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-3-L8.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| *Did* you *charge* your smartphone**?
|===


*Open-ended questions:*

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 先週（せんしゅう）、何（なに）を**しましたか**。 +
 _Senshuu, nani o *shi mashita ka*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-3-L9.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| What *did* you *do* last week**?**

| 日本で何（なに）を**買（か）いましたか**。 +
 _Nihon de nani o *kai mashita ka*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-3-L10.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| What *did* you *buy* in Japan**?**
|===

*NOTE*: The time reference 先週 does not take the particle に because it indicates a relative time frame rather than a specific point in time. For further details on when to use or omit に with time expressions, see <<c06_2, Module 6.2>>.

These examples illustrate how past-tense verbs in the polite form are used in various contexts to express actions and events that occurred in the past.


=== Exercise 1

[[h5p-103]]
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/japanese/wp-admin/admin-ajax.php?action=h5p_embed&id=103[Chapter 12 Past Tense Verbs]

Transform these verbs into their past tense polite form (ました form). Flip the cards to reveal the answers.

[%autowidth]
|===
|食（た）べる | To eat            | [.QA]*食べました*
|話（はな）す | To speak          | [.QA]*話しました*
|書（か）く　 | To write          | [.QA]*書きました*
|泳（およ）ぐ | to swim           | [.QA]*泳ぎました*
|飲（の）む　 | to drink          | [.QA]*飲みました*
|遊（あそ）ぶ | to play/hang out  | [.QA]*遊びました*
|死（し）ぬ　 | to die            | [.QA]*死にました*
|切（き）る　 | to cut            | [.QA]*切りました*
|買（か）う　 | to buy            | [.QA]*書いました*
|持（も）つ　 | to carry/hold     | [.QA]*持ちました*
|来（く）る　 | to come           | [.QA]*来（き）ました*
|する　　　　 | to do             | [.QA]*しました*
|===

You've traveled back in time to ancient Japan. Complete the dialogue using the correct past tense forms in hiragana.

•  *侍（さむらい：Samurai）*: 昨日（きのう）何（なに）を [.QA]*しました* (1:する) か。 +
   What did you do yesterday?
•  *あなた*: 京都（きょうと）でお寺（てら）を [.QA]*みました* (2:見[み]る) 。そして、おいしい豆腐（とうふ）を [.QA]*たべました* (3:食[た]べる) 。 +
   I _saw_ a temple in Kyoto. Then, I _ate_ delicious tofu.
•  *侍*: 富士山（ふじさん）には [.QA]*いきました* (4:行[い]く) か。 +
   Did you _go_ to Mt. Fuji?
•  *あなた*: いいえ、*いきませんでした* (5:行く・negative) 。時間（じかん）が [.QA]*ありませんでした* (6:ある・negative) から。 +
   No, I _didn't go_. Because I _didn't have_ time.

先週（せんしゅう）、大阪（おおさか）に＿＿＿＿。
Last weekend, I went to Osaka.

A. 行きました [.QA]*true*
B. 行きます
C. 行く
D. 行かない

昨日（きのう）友達（ともだち）が学校（がっこう）に＿＿＿＿。
My friend didn't come to school yesterday.

A. 来ました
B. 来ませんでした [.QA]*true*
C. 来る
D. 来ない

富士山（ふじさん）の写真（しゃしん）を＿＿＿＿。
We took many photos of Mt. Fuji.

A. 撮る
B. 撮ります
C. 撮りました [.QA]*true*
D. 撮らない

Drag the words into the correct boxes

1. 日本（にほん）に [.QA]*行きませんでした*。 I didn’t go to Japan.
2. 寿司（すし）を [.QA]*食べませんでした*。 I didn’t eat sushi.
3. 友達（ともだち）と [.QA]*遊びました*。 I played with my friends.
4. 宿題（しゅくだい）を [.QA]*しませんでした*。 I didn’t do my homework.
5. 新（あたら）しい本（ほん）を [.QA]*買いました*。 I bought a new book.


[[c12_4]]
== 12.4 Past Tense of あります (ari masu) and います (i masu)

This module explores how to express the past tense of あります (ari masu) and います (i masu) in Japanese, which are essential for describing the presence or existence of things and people in past contexts.

=== Introduction to the past tense of verbs of existence

In <<c08_4, Module 8.4>>, we covered how to use X があります (X ga ari masu) and X がいます (X ga i masu) to talk about the existence of sentient beings and objects or non-sentient beings (including events), respectively, in the present or future tense.

This module builds on that by showing you how to express these ideas in the past tense.

*Note:* Although this structure is often translated as ‘*There is/are’ or There was/were,* it does not always correspond directly to English usage. For example, while *昨日（きのう）、ここに田中（たなか）さんがいました* (Kinou koko ni Tanaka-san ga imashita) literally means ‘Tanaka-san was here yesterday’, English would typically phrase this as ‘*Tanaka-san was here yesterday’* rather than ‘*There was Tanaka-san here yesterday.’*

Similarly, *先週（せんしゅう）、教室（きょうしつ）に先生（せんせい）がいませんでした* (Senshuu kyoushitsu ni sensei ga imasen deshita) means ‘*There was no teacher in the classroom last week’,* but English speakers would naturally say ‘*There wasn’t a teacher in the classroom last week.’*

=== Usage in sentences

==== 1. Past tense of X があります

In Japanese, X があります is used to indicate the existence of objects, non-sentient beings and events.

*Affirmative past-tense form (There was/were …):* Replace あります with *ありました* to indicate that something existed in the past. For example:

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 昨日（きのう）、お祭（まつ）りが**ありました**。 +
 _Kinou omatsuri ga *ari mashita*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-4-L1.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| *There was* a festival yesterday.
|===


*Negative past-tense form (There wasn’t/weren’t …):* Replace あります with *ありませんでした* to indicate that something did not exist in the past. For example:

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 今日（きょう）、日本語（にほんご）のクラス（くらす）が**ありませんでした**。 +
 _Kinou, Nihongo no kurasu ga *ari masen deshita*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-4-L2.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| *There was* no Japanese class yesterday.
|===


*Questions (Was/Were there …?):* Add the question particle か to ありました to ask whether something existed in the past. For example:

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 日本（にほん）にたくさん面白（おもしろ）い場所（ばしょ）が**ありましたか**。 +
 _Nihon ni takusan omoshiroi basho ga *ari mashita ka.*_
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-4-L3.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| *Were there* many interesting places in Japan**?**
|===


==== 2. Past tense of X がいます

X がいます is used to indicate the existence or presence of sentient beings (people, animals).

*Affirmative past-tense form (There was/were …):* Replace います with *いました* to indicate that a person or animal existed or was present in the past. For example:

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 昨日（きのう）、ここに田中（たなか）さんが**いました**。 +
 _Kinou koko ni Tanaka-san ga *i mashita*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-4-L4.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| Tanaka-san *was* here yesterday.
|===


*Negative past-tense form (There wasn’t/weren’t …):* Replace います with *いませんでした* to indicate that a person or animal existed or was not present in the past. For example:

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 先週（せんしゅう）、教室（きょうしつ）に先生（せんせい）が**いませんでした**。 +
 _Senshuu, kyoushitsu ni sensei ga *i masen deshita.*_
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-4-L5.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| The teacher *was not* in the classroom last week.
|===


*Questions (Was/Were there …?):* Add the question particle か to いました to ask whether a person or animal existed or was present in the past. For example:

[cols=">1a,1a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 東京（とうきょう）にたくさん外国（がいこく）の観光客（かんこうきゃく）が**いましたか。** +
 _Toukyou ni takusan gaikoku no kankoukyaku ga *i mashita ka*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-4-L6.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| *Were there* many tourists from overseas in Tokyo**?**
|===


=== Exercise 1

[[h5p-104]]
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/japanese/wp-admin/admin-ajax.php?action=h5p_embed&id=104[Chapter 12 Arimashita and Imashita]

Transform these verbs into their past tense polite form (ました form). Flip the cards to reveal the answers.

•  あります [.QA]*ありました*
•  ありません [.QA]*ありませんでした*
•  います [.QA]*いました*
•  いません [.QA]*いませんでした*
•  ありますか [.QA]*ありましたか*
•  いますか [.QA]*いましたか*

昨日（きのう）、お祭（まつ）りにたくさんの人（ひと）＿＿＿＿。 There are many people at a festival yesterday.

A. がいます
B. がいました [.QA]*true*
C. がありました
D. がありません

100年前の東京に高層ビル＿＿＿＿。

A. がありました
B. がありませんでした [.QA]*true*
C. がいました
D. がいませんでした

エジプトにピラミッド＿＿＿＿。

A. がありますか
B. がありましたか [.QA]*true*
C. がいますか
D. がいましたか

•  いました is used for people and animals in the past.
   True or False? [.QA]*true*
•  ありませんでした can be used to say there wasn't an event.
   True or False? [.QA]*true*
•  犬（いぬ）がいませんでした means 'there wasn't a dog'.
   True or False? [.QA]*true*
•  本（ほん）がありました can be used to say 'there was a book'.
   True or False? [.QA]*true*
•  先生（せんせい）がありました is correct Japanese.
   True or False? [.QA]*false*
•  新幹線（しんかんせん）がいました is correct Japanese.
   True or False? [.QA]*false*

Drag the words into the correct boxes.
(いました・ありました・いましたか・どうでしたか・ありましたか・いませんでした)

•  A: 昨日（きのう）の図書館（としょかん）は [.QA]*どうでしたか*。 +
   How was the library yesterday?
•  B: とても静（しず）かでした。学生（がくせい）が少（すこ）し [.QA]*いました*。 +
   It was very quiet. There were a few students.
•  A: 新（あたら）しい本（ほん）は [.QA]*ありましたか*。 +
   Was there a new book?
•  B: はい、おもしろい本が [.QA]*ありました*。 +
   Yes, there was an interesting book.
•  A: そうですか。図書館に先生（せんせい）が [.QA]*いましたか*。 +
   I see. Was there a teacher at the library?
•  B: いいえ、先生は [.QA]*いませんでした*。忙（いそが）しかったですから。 +
   No, the teacher wasn’t there because they were busy.

Hints: * おも しろ・い  4 【面白い】（形） * エジプト〖Egypt〗 * ピラミッド  3 〖pyramid〗


[[c12_5]]
== 12.5 Desires in the Past Tense: たかったです (takatta desu)

In this module, we will explore the usage of たかったです (takatta desu), a key structure in Japanese that allows us to express desires and aspirations in the past tense with clarity and nuance.

****
NOTE: 日语动词 「たい」（愿望助动词） 的所有核心时态和肯定/否定变化如下表。「たい」是形容词型活用，所以它的否定和过去变化遵循 「い形容词」的规则，也就是 たい 过去式是 たかった，たくない 过去式是 たくなかった。

[%autowidth%header]
|===
|形态　　　　	|构成　   　　　　　　	|含义　　	|时态　　	|简体例句
|たい　　　　	|连用形 + たい　　　　	|想做　　	|现在肯定	|食べたい
|たい＋ない　	|连用形 + たくない　　	|不想做　	|现在否定	|食べたくない
|たかった　　	|连用形 + たかった　　	|曾想做　	|过去肯定	|食べたかった
|たくなかった	|连用形 + たくなかった	|曾不想做	|过去否定	|食べたくなかった
|===
****

=== Introduction to desires in the past tense: たかったです (takatta desu)

In <<c09_2, Module 9.2>>, we learned how to express desires in the present (or non-past) tense. Now, in this module, we will explore how to express desires in the past tense using the ending pattern *たかったです (takatta desu).*

This form is essential for talking about things you wanted to do or experience in the past. Whether it’s reflecting on a trip to Japan or recalling past aspirations, this structure allows us to do so with clarity!


=== How to form たかったです

==== 1. Affirmative past-tense form (wanted to …)

*たかったです* is the *affirmative past-tense form* of *たいです* (want to). To conjugate a verb into *たかったです*, follow these steps:

•  Start with the *ます* form of the verb.*
*  Example: *食べます* (_tabemasu_) – ‘I eat’.
•  Replace *ます* with *たかったです* to express past desire.
*  Example: *食べたかったです* (_tabetakatta desu_) – ‘I wanted to eat’.

This pattern applies to all verbs that use the *たい form* to express desire.


Alternatively, you can go directly from the *ます* form by replacing *ます* with *たかったです*. For example, *食べます* becomes *食べたかったです*.

*Examples:*

[%autowidth,cols="^,^",frame="ends"]
|===
1.4+<.^| *Ru-verbs*
| 食（た）べます (tabe masu: [I] eat)
|                 ↓
| 食べ**たかったです** (tabe *takatta desu*)
| I *wanted to* eat.

1.4+<.^| *U-verbs*
| 行（い）きます (iki masu: [I] go)
|                 ↓
| 行き**たかったです** (iki *takatta desu*)
| I *wanted to* go.

1.4+<.^| *Irregular verbs*
| します (shi masu: [I] do)
|                 ↓
| し**たかったです** (shi *takatta desu*)
| I *wanted to* do.
|===


==== 2. Negative past-tense form (didn’t want to …) 

*たくなかったです* is the *past negative form* of *たいです* (want to). To conjugate a verb into *たくなかったです*, follow these steps:

•  Start with the *ます* form of the verb.
*  Example: *食べます* (_tabemasu_) – ‘I eat’.
•  Replace *ます* with *たくなかったです* to express past negative desire.
*  Example: *食べたくなかったです* (_tabetakunakatta desu_) – ‘I didn’t want to eat’.

This pattern applies to all verbs that use the *たい form* to express desire in the past negative.


Alternatively, you can go directly from the *ます* form by replacing *ます* with *たくなかったです*. For example, *食べます* becomes *食べたくなかったです*.

*Examples:*

[%autowidth,cols="^,^",frame="ends"]
|===
1.4+<.^| *Ru-verbs*
|食（た）べます (tabe masu: [I] eat)
|        ↓
|食べ**たくなかったです** (tabe *taku nakatta* *desu*)
|I *didn’t want to* eat.

1.4+<.^| *U-verbs*
|行（い）きます (iki masu: [I] go)
|        ↓
|行き**たくなかったです** (iki *taku nakatta* *desu*)
|I *didn’t want to* go.

1.4+<.^| *Irregular-verbs*
|します (shi masu: [I] do)
|        ↓
|し**たくなかったです** (shi *taku nakatta* *desu*)
|I *didn’t want to* do.
|===


==== 3. Questions in the past tense (Did X want to …?)

To form a question in the past tense, *たかったですか (takatta desu ka)*, add the question particle か to たかったです.

*Examples:*

[%autowidth,cols="^,^",frame="ends"]
|===
1.4+<.^|*Ru-verbs*
|食べる (taberu: to eat)
|        ↓
|X は食べ**たかったですか** (tabe *takatta desu ka*)
|Did X *want to* eat**?**

1.4+<.^|*U-verbs*
|行く (iku: to go)
|        ↓
|X は行き**たかったですか** (iki *takatta desu ka*)
|Did X *want to* go**?**

1.4+<.^|*Irregular-verbs*
|する (suru: to do)
|        ↓
|X はし**たかったですか** (shi *takatta desu ka*)
|Did X *want to* do (it)*?*
|===

=== Usage in sentences

Use *たかったです* to express past desires, often implying that they were not fulfilled. It conveys a sense of regret or disappointment about something you wanted to do but couldn’t.

*Examples:*

==== 1. Affirmative past-tense form: たかったです

[cols=">5a,4a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 日本（にほん）で富士山（ふじさん）を見（み）*たかったです*。 +
 _Nihon de Fuji san o mi *takatta desu*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-5-L1.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| I *wanted to* see Mt. Fuji in Japan.
|===


*NOTE:* This sentence suggests that the speaker *wanted to see Mt. Fuji but was unable to do so*.


==== 2. Negative past-tense form: たくなかったです

[cols=">5a,4a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| 子（こ）どもの時（とき）、野菜（やさい）を食（た）べ**たくなかったです**。 +
 _Kodomo no toki, yasai o tabe *taku nakatta desu*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-5-L2.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| When I was a child, I *didn’t want to* eat vegetables.
|===


*NOTE:* This implies that the speaker *actively disliked eating vegetables in childhood*.


==== 3. Questions in the past tense: たかったですか

*Closed-ended question:*

[cols=">5a,4a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| A: 北海道（ほっかいどう）で温泉（おんせん）に入（はい）り**たかったですか**。 +
 _Hokkaidou de onsen ni hairi *takatta desu ka*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-5-L3.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| *Did* you *want to* bathe in [literally, ‘go into’] a hot spring in Hokkaido**?**
|===


[cols=">5a,4a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| B: はい、入り**たかったです**。/ いいえ、入り**たくなかったです**。 +
 _Hai, hairi *takatta desu*. / Iie, hairi *taku nakatta desu*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-5-L4.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| Yes, I *did*. / No, I *didn’t*.
|===


*NOTE:* The affirmative response *may imply that the speaker wanted to but couldn’t*, while the negative response simply states that they had no desire to do so.


*Open-ended question:*

[cols=">5a,4a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| A: オーストラリアで何（なに）をし**たかったですか**。 +
 _Oosutoraria de nani o shi *takatta desu ka*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-5-L5.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| What did you *want to* do in Australia**?**
|===


[cols=">5a,4a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
| B: コアラに会（あ）い**たかったです**。 +
 _Koara ni ai *takatta desu*._
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/app/uploads/sites/67/2024/05/Chap12-5-L6.m4a[icon:play-circle[]]
| I *wanted to* meet a koala.
|===


*NOTE:* This suggests that *the speaker had a desire to meet a koala, which was likely but not necessarily unfulfilled*.


*NOTE:* To imply that the *desire was fulfilled* or to provide *an explanation in conversation*, *たかったんです* (a more colloquial and explanatory form, or *のです* in formal writing) is often used.

If you want to clearly indicate that the desire was fulfilled, you might say:

[cols=">5a,4a",frame=all,grid=none]
|===
|✔ *日本で富士山を見ました。すごく見たかったんです！* +
 _Nihon de Fuji-san o mi mashita. Sugoku mitakatta n desu._
|I saw Mt. Fuji in Japan. I really wanted to see it!

|✖ *日本で富士山を見たかったです。でも、見ました！* +
 _Nihon de Fuji-san o mitakatta desu. Demo, mi mashita!_
|I wanted to see Mt. Fuji in Japan. But I saw it!
|===

This sounds unnatural because *たかったです* usually implies that the desire was unfulfilled.


=== Summary

This table compares the different conjugations of *たいです*, which is used to express *desire* in Japanese. The table shows how to form the *affirmative and negative* versions in both the *present and past tense*, with example sentences for clarity:

[cols=",,,",options="autowidth,header",]
|===
|*Tense* |*Ending* |*Meaning* |*Example* (食べる – ‘to eat’)
|*Affirmative Present* |*たいです* (_tai desu_) |want to |*食べたいです* (_tabetai desu_) – ‘I want to eat’.
|*Negative Present* |*たくないです* (_takunai desu_) |don’t/doesn’t want to |*食べたくないです* (_tabetakunai desu_) – ‘I don’t want to eat’.
|*Affirmative Past* |*たかったです* (_takatta desu_) |wanted to |*食べたかったです* (_tabetakatta desu_) – ‘I wanted to eat’.
|*Negative Past* |*たくなかったです* (_takunakatta desu_) |didn’t want to |*食べたくなかったです* (_tabetakunakatta desu_) – ‘I didn’t want to eat’.
|===


=== Exercise 1

[[h5p-105]]
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/japanese/wp-admin/admin-ajax.php?action=h5p_embed&id=105[Chapter 12 Past Desire]

Transform these verbs into their past desire form (たかったです form). Flip the cards to reveal the answers.

[%autowidth]
|===
|食（た）べる | (+ たい) wanted to eat ➔   |[.QA]*食べたかったです*
|行（い）く　 | (+ たい) wanted to go ➔    |[.QA]*行きたかったです*
|する　　　　 | (+ たい) wanted to do ➔    |[.QA]*したかったです*
|買（か）う　 | (+ たい) wanted to buy ➔   |[.QA]*買いたかったです*
|会（あ）う　 | (+ たい) wanted to meet ➔  |[.QA]*会いたかったです*
|===

たかったです is used to express current desires.
True or False? [.QA]*False*

The negative form of たかったです is たくなかったです。
True or False? [.QA]*True*

You can use たかったです with all types of verbs.
True or False? [.QA]*True*

たかったですか is used to ask about someone's past desires.
True or False? [.QA]*True*

したくなかったです means 'I didn't do it'.
True or False? [.QA]*False*

子（こ）どもの時（とき）、先生（せんせい）に ＿＿＿＿ 。

A. なりたいです
B. なりたかったです [.QA]*ture*
C. なります
D. なりました

昨日（きのう）のパーティーに ＿＿＿＿ 。

A. 行きたいです
B. 行きたかったです [.QA]*ture*
C. 行きます
D. 行きました [.QA]*ture*

日本語（にほんご）を ＿＿＿＿ 。でも、時間（じかん）がありませんでした。

A. 勉強したいです
B. 勉強したかったです [.QA]*ture*
C. 勉強します
D. 勉強しました

Complete the sentences with the negative past desire form (たくなかったです).

1. 子どもの時、野菜（やさい）を食 [.QA]*べたくなかったです*。(食べる) +
   When I was a child, I didn't want to eat vegetables.
2. 昨日（きのう）は疲（つか）れましたから、勉強（べんきょう） [.QA]*したくなかったです*。(勉強する) +
   Because I was tired yesterday, I didn't want to study.
3. その映画（えいが）は怖（こわ）いですから、見（み） [.QA]*たくなかったです* 。(見る) +
   Because that movie is scary, I didn't want to watch it.
4. そのかばんは高（たか）かったですから、買（か） [.QA]*いたくなかったです*。(買う) +
   Because that bag was expensive, I didn't want to buy it.

Complete the dialogue by choosing the correct question form (たかったですか) and appropriate responses.

•  *You*: 子（こ）どもの時（とき）、どんな仕事（しごと）を [.QA]*したかったですか*。
•  *Your Japanese friend*: ゲームを作（つく）[.QA]*りたかったです*。でも、今（いま）は北海道（ほっかいどう）で野菜（やさい）を [.QA]*作りたいです*。
•  *You*: へえ、そうですか！外国（がいこく）に [.QA]*行きたいですか*。
•  *Your Japanese friend*: うーん、外国にはあまり [.QA]*行きたくないです*。
•  *You*: どうしてですか。
•  *Your Japanese friend*:　英語（えいご）が難（むず）しいですから。
•  *You*:　だいじょうぶですよ！英語を教（おし）えますから。オーストラリアに来（き）てください。
•  *Your Japanese friend*: ありがとう。ぜひ、 [.QA]*行きたいです*！


NOTE: たかったです is the past tense form used to express past desires, not current ones. For current desires, you would use たいです."

NOTE: To negate past desires, you change たかったです to たくなかったです. For example, 行きたかったです (I wanted to go) becomes 行きたくなかったです (I didn’t want to go)."

NOTE: たかったです can be used with all verb types: る-verbs, う-verbs, and irregular verbs. The verb stem is used before たかったです for all verb types."

NOTE: By adding the question particle か to たかったです, you form a question about past desires. For example, 行きたかったですか？ means ’Did you want to go?’"

NOTE: したくなかったです means “I didn’t want to do it.” It expresses a past lack of desire to do something, not the fact of not doing it. To say ’I didn’t do it’, you would use しませんでした."

NOTE: 子（こ）どもの時（とき）、先生（せんせい）になりたかったです。“小时候，我想成为老师。”「なりたかったです」 = なり（“成为”的连用形）+ たい（想）+ たかった（过去）+ です（敬体）。に 表示“成为”的对象。


[[c12_6]]
== 12.6 Review

In this chapter, you have honed your skills in using the past tense in Japanese, focusing on essential grammatical points and daily expressions, including:

{TICK} *_Learned more kanji_*
You’ve expanded your knowledge of the kanji for 天 (heaven), 気 (spirit), 元 (origin), 外 (outside) and 国 (country).

{TICK} *_Familiarised yourself with the past tense of nouns and adjectives_*
You have learned how to conjugate nouns and adjectives into their past-tense forms, enabling you to describe past experiences and qualities.

{TICK} *_Mastered the past tense of verbs_*
You can now effectively use verbs in the past tense, allowing you to talk about actions that occurred in the past.

{TICK} *_Gained proficiency in the past tense of あります and います_*
You have mastered the past-tense forms of あります and います, enabling you to describe the existence or presence of objects, events, people and animals in the past.

{TICK} *_Learned to express desires using the past tense of たいです_*
You can now express desires in the past tense using たかったです, expanding your ability to convey what you wanted to do.

These skills will add so much to your Japanese conversations, and with regular practice, they’ll start to feel natural.


To help you lock in everything you’ve learned in this chapter, we’ve put together a fun revision exercise. Whether you’re looking to brush up on your skills or test your understanding, this exercise is here for you. If you find anything tricky, don’t hesitate to revisit the chapter for a refresher.

=== Exercise 1 

[[h5p-114]]
https://oercollective.caul.edu.au/japanese/wp-admin/admin-ajax.php?action=h5p_embed&id=114[Chapter 12 Review]


Which kanji means 'weather'?

A. 元
B. 天気 [.QA]*true*
C. 外国
D. 天

What is the past tense of the i-adjective 暑（あつ）い?

A. 暑いでした
B. 暑かったです [.QA]*true*
C. 暑くなかったです
D. 暑いです

What is the correct past tense form of あります?

A. ありませんでした
B. あった
C. ありました  [.QA]*true*
D. あっています

What is the correct way to say 'I wanted to drink coffee' in Japanese?

A. コーヒーを飲みたいでした。
B. コーヒーを飲みたかったです。 [.QA]*true*
C. コーヒーを飲みたくなかったです。
D. コーヒーを飲みました。

Drag the words into the correct boxes.
(Origi・Weathe・Foreign countries)

A. 天気 (てんき) = [.QA]*Weather*
B. 元 (げん) = [.QA]*Origin*
C. 外国 (がいこく) = [.QA]*Foreign countries*

Fill in the missing word in hiragana. Complete the sentence using the past tense: 

'Yesterday's weather was hot’. 
Answer: 昨日（きのう）の天気（てんき）は涼（すず）し [.QA]*かった* です。

The past tense of the na-adjective きれいな is きれいじゃないでした. 
True or False?  [.QA]*It’s false. The past tense of きれいな is きれいじゃなかったです.*

Drag the words into the correct boxes. Match the adjectives with their correct past tense forms. 

A. 寒（さむ）い = [.QA]*寒かったです*
B. 面白（おもしろ）い = [.QA]*おもしろかったです*
C. 楽（たの）しい = [.QA]*楽しかったです*

Fill in the missing word in hiragana.
Complete the sentence using the past tense of います:

'There was a teacher and my friends in the room'. 
Answer: クラスに先生（せんせい）と友達（ともだち）が [.QA]*いました*。

Drag the words into the correct boxes. Match the English sentence with its correct past tense form in Japanese.

A. I wanted to read a book. = *本（ほん）を読（よ）みたかったです*。
B. I didn’t want to go. = *行（い）きたくなかったです*。
C. I wanted to watch a movie. = *映画（えいが）を見（み）たかったです*。

Fill in the missing word in hiragana.

Complete the sentence:  'Last year, I wanted to go to Japan'. 
Answer: 去年（きょねん）、日本（にほん）に行（い）き [.QA]*たかった* です。

[[review]]
== Peer-Review Statement

Monash University is committed to publishing high-quality open textbooks which meet the needs of students and educators.  This book has been peer-reviewed by two subject experts from two higher education institutions. Each chapter received a double-blind review from academics with specialist knowledge and experience in Japanese language education.

Reviews were structured around considerations of the intended audience of the book and examined the comprehensiveness, accuracy and relevance of the content. Reviews were also focused on relevance longevity, clarity, consistency, organisation, structure flow, grammatical errors and cultural relevance.

The author and the publication team would like to thank the reviewers for the time, care and commitment they contributed to the project. We recognise that peer reviewing is a generous act of service on their part. This book would not be the robust, valuable resource that it is were it not for their feedback and input.


[[versioning]]
== Versioning History

This page provides a record of edits and changes made to this book since its initial publication. Whenever edits or updates are made in the text, we provide a record and description of those changes here. If the change is minor, the version number increases by 0.1. If the edits involve substantial updates, the version number increases to the next full number.

Scope note: Version notes are limited to changes in content. Corrections to spelling, grammar, the fixing of broken links etc. will not warrant a version note. As this book is being published in two parts, the initial publication of the new section will not trigger a version note.
